agents collaborate to complete a common task. We introduced Federated TD-learning in both on-policy (with function approximation) and off-policy (in tabular and function approximation) settings, as well as federated
Q
-learning. In these algorithms, each agent independently follows a Markovian trajectory of state-action-reward and updates its parameters locally. To ensure convergence, agents synchronize their parameters after every
K
iterations. Our results demonstrate linear speedup across all algorithms with respect to the number of agents. Notably, in off-policy TD-learning with linear function approximation, the communication complexity is
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{{\rm NewA}}/\sqrt{\epsilon})
, while for the other algorithms it remains
\mathcal{O}({\rm NewA})
.

Appendices

The appendices are organized as follows. In Section
A
, we discuss the lower bound on the convergence of a general stochastic approximation.
In Section
B
we derive the convergence bound of the FeGSAM algorithm. Next, we employ the results in Section
B
to derive the convergence bounds of federated TD-learning in Section
C
and federated
Q
-learning in Section
D
.

Appendix A
Lower Bound on the Convergence of General Stochastic Approximation

In this section, we discuss the convergence of the general stochastic approximation. In this discussion, we provide a simple stochastic approximation with i.i.d. noise, which can give insight into the general convergence bound in (
2
). In particular, we show that the convergence bound in (
2
) is tight and cannot be improved.

Consider a one-dimensional random variable
X
with zero mean
{\mathbb{E}}[X]=0
and bounded variance
{\mathbb{E}}[X^{2}]=\sigma^{2}
. Consider the following update

\displaystyle x_{t+1}=x_{t}+\alpha(X_{t}-x_{t}),\quad t\geq 0,

(23)

where we start with some fixed deterministic
x_{0}
and
X_{t}
is a an i.i.d. sample of the random variable
X
. It is easy to see that the update (
23
) is a special case of the update of the general stochastic approximation with the fixed point
x^{*}=0
.

By expanding the update (
23
), we have

\displaystyle x_{t}=(1-\alpha)^{t}x_{0}+\alpha\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-k-1}X_{k}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle x_{t}^{2}=(1-\alpha)^{2t}x_{0}^{2}+\left(\alpha\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-k-1}X_{k}\right)^{2}+2\alpha(1-\alpha)^{t}x_{0}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-k-1}X_{k}.

Taking expectation on both sides, and using the zero mean property of
X_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[x_{t}^{2}]=

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{2t}x_{0}^{2}+\alpha^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-k-1}X_{k}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{2t}x_{0}^{2}+\alpha^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{2(t-k-1)}X_{k}^{2}+\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,k\neq k^{\prime}}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{2t-k-k^{\prime}-2}X_{k}X_{k}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{2t}x_{0}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{2(t-k-1)}\sigma^{2}+\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,k\neq k^{\prime}}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{2t-k-k^{\prime}-2}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{k}]{\mathbb{E}}[X_{k}^{\prime}]\right)

(i.i.d. property)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{2t}x_{0}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{2(t-k-1)}\sigma^{2}\right)

(zero mean)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{2t}x_{0}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}\frac{1-(1-\alpha)^{2t}}{1-(1-\alpha)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{0}^{2}(1-\alpha)^{2t}+\sigma^{2}\frac{1-(1-\alpha)^{2t}}{2-\alpha}\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{(x_{0}^{2}-\frac{\alpha\sigma^{2}}{2-\alpha})(1-\alpha)^{2t}}_{T_{1}:\text{bias}}+\underbrace{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2-\alpha}\alpha}_{T_{2}:\text{variance}}.

(24)

It is clear that (
24
) has the same form as the bound in (
2
) with
T_{1}
as the geometric term which converges to zero as
t\rightarrow\infty
, and
T_{2}
term proportional to the step size
\alpha
. In addition, note that in the above derivation, we did not use any inequality and therefore the bounds in (
24
) and (
2
) are tight.

Appendix B
Analysis of Federated Stochastic Approximation

First, we restate Theorem

3.1
with explicit expressions of the different constants.

Theorem B.1
.

Consider the FeGSAM Algorithm
1
with
c_{FSAM}=1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}
(
{\varphi}_{2}
is defined in (
28
)) and suppose that Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,
3.3
,
3.4
are satisfied. Consider a sufficiently small step size
\alpha
that satisfies the assumptions in (
35
), (
37
), (
46
),
(
49
). Furthermore, denote
\tau_{\alpha}=\lceil 2\log_{\rho}\alpha\rceil
, and take large enough
T
such that
T>\max\{K+\tau_{\alpha},2\tau_{\alpha}\}
. Then, the output of the FeGSAM Algorithm
1
,
{\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}}\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}{\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}}^{i}
, satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}}\|_{c}^{2}]\leq\mathcal{C}_{1}\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau_{\alpha}+1}+\mathcal{C}_{2}\frac{\alpha\tau_{\alpha}^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}+\mathcal{C}_{3}(K-1)\alpha^{2}\tau_{\alpha}+\mathcal{C}_{4}(K-1)^{2}\varepsilon\alpha^{2}\tau_{\alpha}+\mathcal{C}_{5}\alpha^{3}\tau_{\alpha}^{2},

(25)

where
\mathcal{C}_{1}=16u_{cm}^{2}M_{0}(\log_{\rho}\frac{1}{e}+\frac{1}{{\varphi}_{2}})
,
M_{0}=\frac{1}{l_{cm}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{C_{1}^{2}}\left(B+(A_{2}+1)\left(\|{\bm{\theta}}_{0}\|_{c}+\frac{B}{2C_{1}}\right)\right)^{2}+\|{\bm{\theta}}_{0}\|_{c}^{2}\right)
,
\mathcal{C}_{2}=8u_{cm}^{2}\left(C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}+C_{12}\right)/{\varphi}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{3}=80\tilde{B}^{2}C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}\right)/{\varphi}_{2}
,
\mathcal{C}_{4}=\frac{320u_{cm}^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}C_{17}m_{3}}{{\varphi}_{2}B}
and
\mathcal{C}_{5}=8u_{cm}^{2}\left(C_{7}+C_{11}+0.5C_{3}^{2}C_{9}^{2}+C_{3}C_{10}+2C_{1}C_{3}C_{10}+3A_{1}C_{3}+C_{13}+C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}2m^{2}_{2}\alpha^{2}\right)/{\varphi}_{2}
. Here
u_{cm},l_{cm},{\varphi}_{2},C_{1},C_{3},C_{7},C_{8},C_{9},C_{10},C_{11},C_{12},C_{13},C_{17},\tilde{B}
are problem-dependent constants which are defined in the following proposition and lemmas.

Next, we characterize the sample complexity of the FeGSAM Algorithm
1
, where we establish a linear speedup in the convergence of the algorithm.

Corollary B.1.1
.

Consider FeGSAM Algorithm
1
with fixed number of iterations
T
and step size
\alpha=\frac{8\log({\rm NewA}T)}{{\varphi}_{2}T}
. Suppose
T
is large enough, such that
\alpha
satisfies the requirements of the step size in Theorem
B.1
and
T>\max\{4\tau_{\alpha},N\}
. Furthermore, for the choice of
K
, assume

•

if
\varepsilon>0
, take
K=\sqrt{T}/{\rm NewA}
.

•

if
\varepsilon=0
, take
K=T/{\rm NewA}
.

Then we have

{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}}\|_{c}^{2}]\leq\epsilon
after
T=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}\epsilon}\right)
iterations.

Corollary
B.1.1
establishes the sample and communication complexity of the FeGSAM Algorithm
1
. The
\mathcal{O}(1/({\rm NewA}\epsilon))
sample complexity shows the linear speedup with increasing
{\rm NewA}
. Another aspect of the cost is the number of communications required between the agents and the central server. According to Corollary
B.1.1
, when
\varepsilon>0
, we need
T/K=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sqrt{N}/\sqrt{\epsilon})
number of communications. Furthermore, when
\varepsilon=0
, we need
T/K=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}({\rm NewA})
rounds of communication in order to reach an
\epsilon
-optimal solution. Hence, even in the presence of Markov noise, in the homogeneous setting, the required number of communications is independent of the desired final accuracy
\epsilon
, and grows linearly with the number of agents. Our result generalizes the existing result achieved for the simpler i.i.d. noise case in
[
39
,
74
]
.

In the following sections, we discuss the proof of Theorem
B.1
. In Section
B.1
, we introduce some notation and preliminary results to facilitate our analysis based on the Lyapunov function. Next, in Section
B.2
, we state some primary propositions, which are then used to prove Theorem
B.1
. In Sections
B.3
,
B.4
,
B.5
, we prove the aforementioned propositions. Along the way, we state several intermediate results, which are stated and proved in Sections
B.6
and
B.7
, respectively.

Throughout the appendix we have several sets of constants. The constants
C_{i},i=1,\dots,17
are problem-dependent constants which we define recursively. The final constants that appear in the resulting bound in Theorem
B.1
are shown as
\mathcal{C}_{i},i=1,\dots,4
. Finally, the constant
c
is used in the sampling of the time step
\hat{T}
.

B.1
Preliminaries

We define the following notation:

•

{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}}
: virtual sequence of average (across agents) parameter.

•

\bm{\Theta}_{t}=\left\{{\bm{\theta}}^{1}_{t},\ldots,{\bm{\theta}}^{\rm NewA}_{t}\right\}
: set of local parameters at individual nodes.

•

{\mathbf{Y}_{t}}=\left\{{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\rm NewA}\right\}
: Markov chains at individual nodes.

•

\mu^{i}
: the stationary distribution of
{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

•

\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})
: average of the noisy local operators at the individual local parameters.

•

\mathbf{G}({\bm{\theta}_{t}},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})
: average of the noisy local operators at the average parameter.

•

\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\mathbf{b}^{i}({\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})
: average of Markovian noise.

•

\bar{\mathbf{G}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t})\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\bar{\mathbf{G}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}})
: average of the expected operators evaluated at the local parameter.

•

\bar{\mathbf{G}}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\bar{\mathbf{G}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})
: average of the expected operator evaluated at the average parameter.

•

{\bm{\theta}}^{i,*}
is the unique fixed point that satisfies
{\bm{\theta}}^{i,*}=\bar{\mathbf{G}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}}^{i,*})
for all
i=1,\dots,{\rm NewA}
. Note that this follows directly from the assumptions. In particular, by Assumption
3.2
,
\bar{\mathbf{G}}^{i}(\cdot)
is a contraction, and hence by the Banach fixed point theorem
[
1
]
, there exists a unique fixed point of this operator.

•

The vector
\mathbf{0}
is the unique fixed point that satisfies
\bar{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{0})=\mathbf{0}
. Note that the existence and uniqueness of this fixed point is due to the contractive property of
\bar{\mathbf{G}}(\cdot)
and the Banach fixed point theorem.

•

\Delta_{t}^{i}=\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}}\|_{c},\quad\Delta_{t}=\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\Delta_{t}^{i},\quad\Omega_{t}=\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}(\Delta_{t}^{i})^{2}
: measures of the synchronization error.

Throughout this proof, we assume
\|\cdot\|_{c}
as some given norm.
{\mathbb{E}}_{t}[\cdot]\triangleq{\mathbb{E}}[\cdot|\mathcal{F}_{t}]
, where
\mathcal{F}_{t}
is the sigma algebra generated by
\{{\bm{\theta}}_{r}^{i}\}_{r=1,\dots,t}^{i=1,\dots,{\rm NewA}}
. Unless otherwise specified,
\|\cdot\|
denotes the Euclidean norm.

Generalized Moreau Envelope:

Consider the norm
\|\cdot\|_{c}
which appears in Assumptions
3.1
-
3.3
. The square of this norm does not need to be smooth. Inspired by
[
16
]
, we use the Generalized Moreau Envelope as a Lyapunov function for the analysis of the convergence of Algorithm
1
. The Generalized Moreau Envelope of
f(\cdot)
with respect to
g(\cdot)
, for
\psi>0
, is defined as

\displaystyle{M}_{f}^{\psi,g}({\mathbf{x}})=\min_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\{f({\mathbf{u}})+\frac{1}{\psi}g({\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{u}})\right\}.

(26)

Let
f({\mathbf{x}})=\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{x}}\|_{c}^{2}
and
g({\mathbf{x}})=\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{x}}\|_{s}^{2}
, which is
L
-smooth with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{s}
norm. For this choice of
f,g
,
{M}_{f}^{\psi,g}(\cdot)
is essentially a smooth approximation to
f
, which is henceforth denoted with the simpler notation
{M}(\cdot)
. Also, due to the equivalence of norms, there exist
l_{cs},u_{cs}>0
such that

\displaystyle l_{cs}\|\cdot\|_{s}\leq\|\cdot\|_{c}\leq u_{cs}\|\cdot\|_{s}.

(27)

We next summarize the properties of
M(\cdot)
in the following proposition, which were established in
[
16
]
.

Proposition B.1

(
[
17
]
)
.

The function
{M}(\cdot)
satisfies the following properties.

1.

{M}(\cdot)
is convex, and
\frac{L}{\psi}
-smooth with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{s}
. That is,
{M}({\mathbf{y}})\leq{M}({\mathbf{x}})+\langle\nabla{M}({\mathbf{x}}),{\mathbf{y}}-{\mathbf{x}}\rangle+\frac{L}{2\psi}\|{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}\|_{s}^{2}
for all
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

2.

There exists a norm, denoted by
\|\cdot\|_{m}
, such that
{M}({\mathbf{x}})=\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{x}}\|_{m}^{2}
.

3.

Let
\ell_{cm}=(1+\psi\ell_{cs}^{2})^{1/2}
and
u_{cm}=(1+\psi u_{cs}^{2})^{1/2}
. Then we have
\ell_{cm}\|\cdot\|_{m}\leq\|\cdot\|_{c}\leq u_{cm}\|\cdot\|_{m}
.

By Proposition
B.1
, we can use
M(\cdot)
as a smooth surrogate for
\frac{1}{2}\|\cdot\|_{c}^{2}
. Furthermore, we denote

\displaystyle{\varphi}_{1}=\frac{1+\psi u_{cs}^{2}}{1+\psi\ell_{cs}^{2}},\quad{\varphi}_{2}=1-\gamma_{c}{\varphi}_{1}^{1/2},\quad\text{and}\quad{\varphi}_{3}=\frac{114L(1+\psi u_{cs}^{2})}{\psi\ell_{cs}^{2}}.

(28)

Note that by choosing
\psi>0
small enough, we can ensure
{\varphi}_{2}\in(0,1)
.

B.2
Proof of Theorem
B.1

In this section, first we state three key results (Propositions
B.2
,
B.3
,
B.4
). These are then used to prove Theorem
B.1
.
The first step of the proof is to characterize the one-step drift of the Lyapunov function
M(\cdot)
, with the parameters generated by the FeGSAM Algorithm
1
, which is formally stated in the following proposition.

Proposition B.2

(One-step drift - I)
.

Consider the update of the FeGSAM Algorithm
1
. Suppose that Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,
3.3
, and
3.4
are satisfied. Consider
\tau=\lceil 2\log_{\rho}\alpha\rceil
and
t\geq 2\tau
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1-\alpha\left[2{\varphi}_{2}-\zeta_{1}^{2}-\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{2LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}-\zeta_{3}^{2}\frac{L(A_{1}+1)}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}-\zeta_{4}^{2}\right]+\alpha^{2}\frac{6Lu_{cm}^{2}}{l_{cs}^{2}\psi}\left[m_{1}+\frac{(A_{2}+1)^{2}}{2}\right]\bigg){\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle+\alpha^{2}\underbrace{\left(\frac{m_{4}L}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\right)}_{C_{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}\right]

(29)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{L}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\left(\frac{L}{2\psi l_{cs}^{2}}+\alpha A_{2}\left(1+\tfrac{u_{cm}^{2}}{\zeta_{2}^{2}}\right)+\alpha(A_{1}+1)\left(\tfrac{3u_{cm}^{2}}{2\zeta_{3}^{2}}+2\right)+3m_{1}\alpha^{2}\right)}_{C_{4}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}]

(30)

\displaystyle+\alpha\underbrace{\frac{L}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{3u_{cm}^{2}}{2\zeta_{3}^{2}}+\frac{3}{2}\right)(A_{1}+1)+A_{2}+\frac{Lu_{cm}^{2}}{2l_{cs}^{2}\zeta_{4}^{2}\psi}+\frac{3A_{1}^{2}\alpha^{2}}{2}\right)}_{C_{5}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{t}]

(31)

\displaystyle+\alpha\underbrace{\frac{L}{2l_{cs}^{2}\psi}\left(\left(\frac{3u_{cm}^{2}}{\zeta_{3}^{2}}+3\right)(A_{1}+1)+m_{1}\alpha\right)}_{C_{6}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{t-\tau}]

(32)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{m^{2}_{4}u_{cm}^{2}L^{2}}{2\zeta_{1}^{2}l_{cs}^{4}\psi^{2}}\alpha}_{C_{7}}\alpha^{4}

(33)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{3L}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\right)}_{C_{8}}\alpha^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[\|\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}\right],

(34)

where
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{2}
,
\zeta_{3}
, and
\zeta_{4}
are arbitrary positive constants.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
B.2
is presented in full detail in Section
B.3
.
∎

Before discussing the bound in its full generality, we discuss a few special cases.

•

Perfect synchronization
(K=1)
with i.i.d. noise: since
{\Omega_{t}}=0
for all
t
,
\tau=0
(independence across time) and
C_{7}=0
(see Lemmas
B.2
and
B.5
in Section
B.6
), the terms (
29
), (
30
) (
31
),(
32
), (
33
) will not appear in the bound, which is the form we get for centralized systems with i.i.d. noise
[
66
]
.

•

Infrequent synchronization
(K>1)
with i.i.d. noise:
\tau=0
, and
C_{7}=0
, the terms (
29
), (
30
), (
32
), (
33
) will not appear in the bound, which is the form we get for federated stochastic optimization with i.i.d. noise
[
39
]
.

•

Perfect synchronization
(K=1)
with Markov noise: since
{\Omega_{t}}=0
for all
t
, the bound in Proposition
B.2
generalizes the results in
[
75
,
18
]
.

Next, we substitute the bound on
\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}
(Lemma
B.6
) and
\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}
(Lemma
B.7
) to further bound the one-step drift. Establishing a tight bound for these two quantities is essential to ensure linear speedup.

Proposition B.3

(One-step drift - II)
.

Consider the update of the FeGSAM Algorithm
1
. Suppose Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,
3.3
, and
3.4
are satisfied. Define
C_{15}(\tau)=\left(\frac{6m_{1}Lu_{cm}^{2}}{l_{cs}^{2}\psi}+\frac{6L(A_{2}+1)^{2}u_{cm}^{2}}{2\psi l_{cs}^{2}}+144(A_{2}^{2}+1)C_{4}u_{cm}^{2}\right)\tau^{2}
. For

\displaystyle\alpha\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{360\tau(A_{2}^{2}+1)},\frac{{\varphi}_{2}}{2C_{15}(\tau)}\right\},

(35)

\tau=\lceil 2\log_{\rho}\alpha\rceil
, and
t\geq 2\tau
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right]\leq\left(1-\alpha{\varphi}_{2}\right){\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]+C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{4}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}+\alpha C_{17}\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{k}],

where
C_{14}(\tau)=\left(C_{7}+C_{11}+0.5C_{3}^{2}C_{9}^{2}+C_{3}C_{10}+2C_{1}C_{3}C_{10}+3A_{1}C_{3}+C_{13}+C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}2m^{2}_{2}\alpha^{2}\right)\tau^{2}
,
C_{16}(\tau)=\left(C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}+C_{12}\right)\tau^{2}
and
C_{17}=(3A_{1}C_{3}+8A_{1}^{2}C_{4}+C_{5}+C_{6})
. Here we define
C_{9}=\frac{8u_{cD}B}{l_{cD}}
,
C_{10}=\frac{8m_{2}u_{cD}}{l_{cD}(1-\rho)}
,
C_{11}=\frac{8C_{1}^{2}C_{3}^{2}u_{cm}^{2}}{\zeta_{6}^{2}}
,
C_{12}=\frac{8C_{4}u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}
,
C_{13}=\frac{14C_{4}u_{cD}^{2}m_{2}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}(1-\rho^{2})}
.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
B.3
is presented in full detail in Section
B.4
.
∎

Conditional expectation

{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\cdot]
.

Conditional expectation
{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\cdot]
used in Proposition
B.3
is essential when dealing with Markovian noise. The idea of using conditional expectation to deal with Markovian noise is not novel per se. In the previous work
[
10
,
75
,
18
]
, conditioning on
t-\tau
is sufficient to establish the convergence results. Due to the mixing property (Assumption
3.1
), the Markov chain geometrically converges to its stationary distribution. Therefore, choosing “large enough”
\tau
, and conditioning on
t-\tau
, one can ensure that the Markov chain at time
t
is “almost in steady state.” However, in federated setting, conditioning on
t-\tau
results in bounds that are too loose. In particular, consider the differences
\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}
and
\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}
in (
29
) and (
30
), respectively. In the centralized setting, as in
[
10
,
75
,
18
]
, these terms can be deterministically bounded to yield the bound
\asymp\alpha^{2}
. However, in the federated setting, this crude bound does not result in linear speedup in
{\rm NewA}
.
In this work, to achieve a finer bound on
\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}
, we go
\tau
steps further back in time. This ensures that the difference behaves almost like the difference of the average of i.i.d. random variables, resulting in a tighter bound (see Lemma
B.6
). Using the conditional expectation
{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\cdot]
, we derive a refined analysis to bound this term as
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{2}/{\rm NewA}+\alpha^{4})
, which guarantees a linear speedup (see Lemmas
B.6
and
B.7
).

Taking total expectation in Proposition
B.3
(using tower property), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right]\leq\left(1-\alpha{\varphi}_{2}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]+C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{4}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}+\alpha C_{17}\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}[\Omega_{k}]

(36)

To understand the bound in Proposition
B.3
, consider the case of
K=1
(i.e. full synchronization). In this case, we have
\Omega_{i}=0
for all
i
, and the bound in Proposition
B.3
simplifies to
{\mathbb{E}}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right]\leq\left(1-\alpha{\varphi}_{2}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]+C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{3}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}
. This recursion is sufficient to achieve linear speedup. However, the bound in Proposition
B.3
also includes terms that are proportional to the error due to synchronization. In order to ensure convergence along with linear speedup, we need to further upper bound this term with terms which are of the order
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{3}(K-1))
and
M({\bm{\theta}}_{i})
. The following lemma is the next important contribution of the paper, where we establish such a bound for weighted sum of the synchronization error. Notice that the weights
\{w_{t}\}
are carefully chosen to ensure the best rate of convergence for the overall algorithm.

Proposition B.4

(Synchronization Error)
.

Suppose
T>K+\tau
. For
\alpha
such that

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{{\varphi}_{2}^{2}},\frac{\ln(5/4)}{2(1+\tilde{A}_{1})(K-1)^{2}}\right\},

(37)

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}(\log_{\rho}(\alpha)+1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{v}{2K^{2}},

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}(\log_{\rho}(\alpha)+1)^{3}\exp\left(\alpha{\varphi}_{2}\left(2\log_{\rho}\alpha+1\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{v}{4K}\exp\left(-{\varphi}_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\ln(5/4)}{2(1+\tilde{A}_{1})}}\right),

where
v=\frac{{\varphi}_{2}}{80\tilde{A}_{2}C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}
, the weighted consensus error satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\left[\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Omega_{\ell}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\frac{10C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}+\frac{4m_{3}(K-1)}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)(\tau+1)(K-1)+\frac{1}{2W_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}w_{t}\mathbb{E}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}).

(38)

Proof.

The proof is presented in Section
B.5
.
∎

Finally, incorporating the results of Propositions
B.2
,
B.3
, and
B.4
, we can establish the convergence of FeGSAM in Theorem
B.1
.

Proof of Theorem
B.1
.

Assume
w_{0}=1
, and consider the weights
w_{t}
generated by the recursion
w_{t}=w_{t-1}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{-1}
. Multiplying both sides of (
36
) by
\frac{2w_{t}}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}
, and rearranging the terms, we get

\displaystyle w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})\leq\frac{2w_{t}}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right){\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})-\frac{2w_{t}}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2w_{t}}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\left(C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{4}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}\right)+\frac{2w_{t}\alpha C_{17}}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Omega_{\ell}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\left[w_{t-1}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})-w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right]+\frac{2w_{t}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{3}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}\right)+\frac{2w_{t}C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Omega_{\ell}\right],

(39)

where we use
w_{t-1}=w_{t}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)
.
Summing (
39
) over
t=2\tau
to
T
(define
W_{T}=\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\left[w_{2\tau-1}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{2\tau})-w_{T}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{T+1})\right]+\frac{2}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{3}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}\right)\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}\frac{2w_{t}C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left[\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\Omega_{\ell}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2w_{2\tau-1}}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}w_{T}}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{2\tau})+\frac{2}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{3}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}\right)+\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\Omega_{\ell}

(
\because M({\bm{\theta}})\geq 0,W_{T}\geq w_{T}
)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{2\tau})+\frac{2}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{3}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}\right)+\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\left[\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\Omega_{\ell}\right].

(40)

Substituting the bound on
\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\left[\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\Omega_{\ell}\right]
from Proposition
B.4
into (
40
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{2\tau})+\frac{2}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{3}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}\right)

\displaystyle\ +\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\frac{10C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}+\frac{4m_{3}(K-1)}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)(\tau+1)(K-1)

\displaystyle\ +\frac{1}{2W_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}w_{t}\mathbb{E}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}\left[M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})\right]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}M_{0}+\frac{4}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{3}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}\right)

\displaystyle+\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\frac{20C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}+\frac{4m_{3}(K-1)}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)(\tau+1)(K-1)

\displaystyle\ +2\tau\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}M_{0},

(41)

where
M_{0}
is a problem dependent constant and is defined in Lemma
B.6
.
To simplify (
41
), we define
\bar{C}_{18}(\tau)=\frac{40\tilde{B}^{2}C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}\right)\tau
,
\bar{C}_{18}^{\prime}(\tau)=\frac{160\tilde{B}^{2}C_{17}m_{3}}{{\varphi}_{2}B}\tau
,
\bar{C}_{19}(\tau)=\frac{4}{{\varphi}_{2}}C_{16}(\tau)
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}+2\tau\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}M_{0}+\frac{4C_{14}(\tau)}{{\varphi}_{2}}\alpha^{3}

\displaystyle+\bar{C}_{18}(\tau)(K-1)\alpha^{2}+\bar{C}_{18}^{\prime}(\tau)(K-1)^{2}\varepsilon\alpha^{2}+\bar{C}_{19}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}.

(42)

Furthermore, define
\tilde{W}_{T}=\sum_{t=0}^{T}w_{t}\geq W_{T}
. By definition of
\hat{T}
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}[M({\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}})]=\frac{1}{\tilde{W}_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})
, and hence

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[M({\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}})]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{W}_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{2\tau-1}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})+\frac{1}{\tilde{W}_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{0}}{\tilde{W}_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{2\tau-1}w_{t}+\frac{1}{\tilde{W}_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})

(Lemma
B.6
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\tau M_{0}w_{2\tau-1}}{\tilde{W}_{T}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{W}_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})

(
w_{t}\leq w_{t+1}
for all
t\geq 0
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\tau M_{0}w_{2\tau-1}}{w_{T}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{W}_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\tau M_{0}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}+\frac{1}{\tilde{W}_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\tau M_{0}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}+\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}{\mathbb{E}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(4\tau+\frac{4}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\right)M_{0}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}+\frac{4C_{14}(\tau)}{{\varphi}_{2}}\alpha^{3}

\displaystyle+\bar{C}_{18}(\tau)(K-1)\alpha^{2}+\bar{C}_{18}^{\prime}(\tau)(K-1)^{2}\varepsilon\alpha^{2}+\bar{C}_{19}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}

(by (
42
))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{C}_{20}(\alpha,\tau)\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}+\frac{4C_{14}(\tau)}{{\varphi}_{2}}\alpha^{3}

\displaystyle+\bar{C}_{18}(\tau)(K-1)\alpha^{2}+\bar{C}_{18}^{\prime}(\tau)(K-1)^{2}\varepsilon\alpha^{2}+\bar{C}_{19}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}},

where
\bar{C}_{20}(\alpha,\tau)=\left(4\tau M_{0}+\frac{4M_{0}}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}\right)
. Furthermore, by Proposition
B.1
, we have
M({\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}})=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}}\|_{m}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2u_{cm}^{2}}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}}\|_{c}^{2}
, and hence

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{\hat{T}}\|_{c}^{2}]\leq

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{1}(\alpha,\tau)\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}+\mathcal{C}_{2}(\tau)\frac{\alpha}{{\rm NewA}}+\mathcal{C}_{3}(\tau)(K-1)\alpha^{2}+\mathcal{C}_{4}(\tau)(K-1)^{2}\varepsilon\alpha^{2}+\mathcal{C}_{5}(\tau)\alpha^{3}

(43)

where
\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{1}(\alpha,\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\bar{C}_{20}(\alpha,\tau)
,
\mathcal{C}_{2}(\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\bar{C}_{19}(\tau)
,
\mathcal{C}_{3}(\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\bar{C}_{18}(\tau)
,
\mathcal{C}_{4}(\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\bar{C}_{18}^{\prime}(\tau)
and
\mathcal{C}_{5}(\tau)=\frac{8u_{cm}^{2}C_{14}(\tau)}{{\varphi}_{2}}
.

Finally, note that by definition of
\tau
, we have
\tau=\lceil 2\log_{\rho}\alpha\rceil\leq 1+2\log_{\rho}\alpha=1+2(\ln\alpha)(\log_{\rho}e)=1+(2\log_{\rho}\frac{1}{e})\ln\frac{1}{\alpha}\leq 1+(2\log_{\rho}\frac{1}{e})\frac{1}{\alpha}
. Hence, we have
\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{1}(\alpha,\tau)\leq 2u_{cm}^{2}M_{0}(4+\frac{8}{\alpha}\log_{\rho}\frac{1}{e}+\frac{4}{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}})\leq 16u_{cm}^{2}M_{0}(\log_{\rho}\frac{1}{e}+\frac{1}{{\varphi}_{2}})\frac{1}{\alpha}=\mathcal{C}_{1}.\frac{1}{\alpha}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{1}=16u_{cm}^{2}M_{0}(\log_{\rho}\frac{1}{e}+\frac{1}{{\varphi}_{2}})
.

Furthermore, we have
\mathcal{C}_{2}(\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\bar{C}_{19}(\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\frac{4}{{\varphi}_{2}}C_{16}(\tau)=\frac{8u_{cm}^{2}\left(C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}+C_{12}\right)}{{\varphi}_{2}}\tau^{2}\equiv\frac{8u_{cm}^{2}\left(C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}+C_{12}\right)}{{\varphi}_{2}}\tau_{\alpha}^{2}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{2}\tau_{\alpha}^{2}
, where we denote
\tau_{\alpha}\equiv\tau
to emphasize the dependence of
\tau
on
\alpha
, and
\mathcal{C}_{2}=\frac{8u_{cm}^{2}\left(C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}+C_{12}\right)}{{\varphi}_{2}}
. Note that we have
\tau_{\alpha}=\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\alpha))
.

In addition,
\mathcal{C}_{3}(\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\bar{C}_{18}(\tau)=\frac{80\tilde{B}^{2}C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}\right)}{{\varphi}_{2}}.\tau\equiv\mathcal{C}_{3}\tau_{\alpha}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{3}=\frac{80\tilde{B}^{2}C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}\right)}{{\varphi}_{2}}
.

Additionaly,
\mathcal{C}_{4}(\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\bar{C}_{18}^{\prime}(\tau)=2u_{cm}^{2}\frac{160\tilde{B}^{2}C_{17}m_{3}}{{\varphi}_{2}B}\tau\equiv\mathcal{C}_{4}\tau
, where
\mathcal{C}_{4}=\frac{320u_{cm}^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}C_{17}m_{3}}{{\varphi}_{2}B}
.

And lastly,
\mathcal{C}_{5}(\tau)=\frac{8u_{cm}^{2}\left(C_{7}+C_{11}+0.5C_{3}^{2}C_{9}^{2}+C_{3}C_{10}+2C_{1}C_{3}C_{10}+3A_{1}C_{3}+C_{13}+C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}2m^{2}_{2}\alpha^{2}\right)}{{\varphi}_{2}}\tau^{2}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{5}\tau_{\alpha}^{2}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{5}=8u_{cm}^{2}\left(C_{7}+C_{11}+0.5C_{3}^{2}C_{9}^{2}+C_{3}C_{10}+2C_{1}C_{3}C_{10}+3A_{1}C_{3}+C_{13}+C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}2m^{2}_{2}\alpha^{2}\right)/{\varphi}_{2}
.
∎

Next, we will state the proof of Corollary
B.1.1
.

Proof of Corollary
B.1.1
.

By this choice of step size, for large enough
T
,
\alpha
will be small enough and can satisfy the requirements of step size in (
35
), (
37
), (
46
),
(
49
). Furthermore, the first term in (
43
) will be

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1}\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-2\tau+1}\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1}\frac{1}{\alpha}e^{-\frac{4\log({\rm NewA}T)}{T}(T-2\tau+1)}=\mathcal{C}_{1}\frac{{\varphi}_{2}T}{8\log(NT)}e^{-\log(({\rm NewA}T)^{4})(1+\frac{1-2\tau}{T})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{C}_{1}{\varphi}_{2}T}{8\log(NT)}\left(\frac{1}{N^{4}T^{4}}\right)^{(1+\frac{1-2\tau}{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{C}_{1}{\varphi}_{2}T}{8\log(NT)}\left(\frac{1}{N^{4}T^{4}}\right)^{0.5}

(Assumption on
T
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{C}_{1}{\varphi}_{2}T}{8\log(NT)}\frac{1}{N^{2}T^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{C}_{1}{\varphi}_{2}}{NT}\right).

Furthermore, for the second term we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}\frac{\alpha\tau_{\alpha}^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{C}_{2}/{\varphi}_{2}}{{\rm NewA}T}\right).

For the third, and the fourth terms we have

•

For
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{3}(K-1)\alpha^{2}\tau_{\alpha}+\mathcal{C}_{4}(K-1)^{2}\varepsilon\alpha^{2}\tau_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{N}.\frac{1}{T^{2}}+\frac{T}{N^{2}}\varepsilon\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{NT}\right)

•

For
\varepsilon=0
, the fourth term is zero. Hence, for the third term we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{3}(K-1)\alpha^{2}\tau_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{T}{N}.\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{NT})

For the fifth term we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{5}\alpha^{3}\tau_{\alpha}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{T^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{NT})

(
T>N
)

Upper bounding (
43
) with
\epsilon
, we get
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}T}\right)\leq\epsilon
. Hence, we need to have
T=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}\epsilon}\right)
number of iterations to get to a ball around the optimum with radius
\epsilon
.
∎

B.3
Proof of Proposition
B.2

The update of the virtual parameter sequence
\{{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\}
can be written as follows

\displaystyle{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}={\bm{\theta}_{t}}+\alpha\left(\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}+\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right).

(44)

Using
\frac{p-1}{\psi}
-smoothness of
{M}(\cdot)
(Proposition
B.1
), we get

\displaystyle{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})+\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\right\rangle+\frac{L}{2\psi}\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\right\|_{s}^{2}

(Smoothness of
{M}(\cdot)
)

\displaystyle={M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})+\alpha\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}+\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\rangle+\frac{L\alpha^{2}}{2\psi}\left\|\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}+\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\|_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle={M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})+\alpha\underbrace{\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\right\rangle}_{T_{1}:\text{ Expected update}}+\alpha\underbrace{\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\rangle}_{\begin{subarray}{c}T_{2}:\text{ Error due to Markovian }\\
\text{ noise }\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha\underbrace{\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t})\rangle}_{T_{3}:\text{ Error due to Markovian noise }Y_{k}}+\alpha\underbrace{\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})\rangle}_{T_{4}:\text{ Error due local updates}}.

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{L\alpha^{2}}{2\psi}\underbrace{\left\|\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}+\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\|_{s}^{2}}_{T_{5}:\text{ Error due to noise and discretization}}.

(45)

The inequality in (
45
) characterizes the one-step drift of the Lyapunov function
M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})
. The term
T_{1}
is responsible for the negative drift of the overall recursion.
T_{2}
and
T_{3}
appear due to the presence of Markovian noises
\mathbf{b}^{i}({\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})
and
\mathbf{G}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})
in the update of Algorithm
1
.
T_{4}
appears due to the mismatch between the parameters of the agents
{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},i=1,\dots,{\rm NewA}
. Finally,
T_{5}
appears due to the discretization error in the smoothness upper bound. Next, we state bounds on
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3},T_{4},T_{5}
in the following intermediate lemmas.

Lemma B.1
.

For all
{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the operator
{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}})
satisfies the following

\displaystyle T_{1}=\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}),{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}})-{\bm{\theta}}\right\rangle\leq-2{\varphi}_{2}M({\bm{\theta}}).

Lemma
B.1
guarantees the negative drift in the one-step recursion analysis of Proposition
B.2
. This follows from the Moreau envelope construction
[
18
]
and the contraction property of the operators
{\bar{\mathbf{G}}^{i}}(\cdot),i=1,\dots,{\rm NewA}
(Assumption
3.2
).

Lemma B.2
.

Consider the iteration
t
of the Algorithm
1
, and consider
\tau=\lceil 2\log_{\rho}\alpha\rceil
. We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[T_{2}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L^{2}}{2\alpha\psi^{2}l_{cs}^{4}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\left[\|\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\alpha m_{4}L}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{c}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{Lm_{4}\alpha^{2}u_{cm}}{\zeta_{1}l_{cs}^{2}\psi}\right)^{2}+\zeta_{1}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})],

where
\zeta_{1}
is an arbitrary positive constant.

In the i.i.d. noise setting,
\mathbb{E}[T_{2}]=0
. In Markov noise setting, going back
\tau
steps (which introduce
{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}
) enables us to use Markov chain mixing property (Assumption
3.1
).

Lemma B.3
.

For any
t\geq 0
, denote
T_{3}=\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t})\rangle
. For any
\tau<t
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[T_{3}]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{2LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}+\zeta_{3}^{2}\frac{L(A_{1}+1)}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}+\frac{6\alpha m_{1}Lu_{cm}^{2}}{l_{cs}^{2}\psi}\right){\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{2LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{u_{cm}^{2}}{2\zeta_{2}^{2}}\right)+\frac{L(A_{1}+1)}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\left(\frac{3u_{cm}^{2}}{2\zeta_{3}^{2}}+2\right)+\frac{3m_{1}L\alpha}{l_{cs}^{2}\psi}\right){\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\left[\left\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\right\|_{c}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left(\frac{3u_{cm}^{2}}{2\zeta_{3}^{2}}+\frac{3}{2}\right)\cdot\frac{L(A_{1}+1)}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}+\frac{LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\right){\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[\Omega_{t}]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left(\frac{3u_{cm}^{2}}{2\zeta_{3}^{2}}+\frac{3}{2}\right)\frac{L(A_{1}+1)}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}+\frac{m_{1}L\alpha}{2l_{cs}^{2}\psi}\right){\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[\Omega_{t-\tau}],

where
\zeta_{2}
and
\zeta_{3}
are arbitrary positive constants.

Lemma B.4
.

For any
t\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle T_{4}=\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})\rangle\leq\zeta_{4}^{2}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})+\frac{L^{2}u_{cm}^{2}}{2l_{cs}^{4}\zeta_{4}^{2}\psi^{2}}\Omega_{t},

where
\zeta_{4}
is an arbitrary positive constant.

Lemma B.5
.

For any
0\leq\tau<t
, we have

\displaystyle T_{5}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}+\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\|_{s}^{2}\leq\frac{6(A_{2}+1)^{2}u_{cm}^{2}}{l_{cs}^{2}}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})+\frac{3A_{1}^{2}}{l_{cs}^{2}}\Omega_{t}+\frac{3}{l_{cs}^{2}}\|\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}.

Substituting the bounds in Lemmas
B.1
,
B.2
,
B.3
,
B.4
,
B.5
, and taking expectation, we get the final bound in Proposition
B.2
.

B.4
Proof of Proposition
B.3

First, we state the following two intermediate lemmas, which are proved in Section
B.7
.

Lemma B.6
.

Suppose
\tau=\lceil 2\log_{\rho}\alpha\rceil
and

\displaystyle\alpha\tau\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{24\sqrt{A^{2}_{2}+1}},\frac{1}{8(A_{2}+1)}\right\}.

(46)

For any
0\leq t\leq 2\tau
we have the following

\displaystyle M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\leq\frac{1}{l_{cm}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{C_{1}^{2}}\left(B+(A_{2}+1)\left(\|{\bm{\theta}}_{0}\|_{c}+\frac{B}{2C_{1}}\right)\right)^{2}+\|{\bm{\theta}}_{0}\|_{c}^{2}\right)\equiv M_{0}.

(47)

Furthermore, for any
t\geq 2\tau
, we have the following

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}]

\displaystyle\leq 4\alpha\tau C_{1}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{c}]+8\alpha\tau\frac{u_{cD}}{l_{cD}}\frac{B}{\sqrt{{\rm NewA}}}+\frac{u_{cD}}{l_{cD}}\frac{8\sqrt{Bm_{4}}}{1-\sqrt{\rho}}\alpha^{2}

(48)

\displaystyle+6A_{1}\alpha\sum_{i=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Delta_{i}].

Lemma B.7
.

Suppose
\tau=\lceil 2\log_{\rho}\alpha\rceil
and

\displaystyle\alpha\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{C_{1}},\frac{1}{8\tau C_{2}},\frac{1}{40\tau C_{1}^{2}}\right\},

(49)

where
C_{1}=3\sqrt{A^{2}_{2}+1}
and
C_{2}=3C_{1}+8
.
We have the following

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 8\tau^{2}\alpha^{2}C_{1}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{c}^{2}+8\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}\frac{B^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}\alpha^{2}\tau^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+8\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}\frac{Bm_{4}\alpha^{4}\tau}{1-\rho}+8\alpha^{2}A_{1}^{2}\tau\sum_{i=0}^{\tau}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[\Delta_{t-i}^{2}\right].

In Lemma
B.6
, we define
C_{9}\triangleq\frac{8u_{cD}B}{l_{cD}},C_{10}\triangleq\frac{8\sqrt{Bm_{4}}u_{cD}}{l_{cD}(1-\sqrt{\rho})}
. Hence, we can bound the term in (
29
) as

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}C_{3}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}C_{3}\left(4\alpha\tau C_{1}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{c}]+C_{9}\frac{\alpha\tau}{\sqrt{{\rm NewA}}}+C_{10}\alpha^{2}+6A_{1}\alpha\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Delta_{k}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}C_{3}\left(4C_{1}u_{cm}\alpha\tau{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[\sqrt{2M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})}\right]+C_{9}\frac{\alpha\tau}{\sqrt{{\rm NewA}}}+C_{10}\alpha^{2}+6A_{1}\alpha\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Delta_{k}]\right)

(Proposition
B.1
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\zeta_{6}}4C_{1}C_{3}u_{cm}\alpha^{5/2}\tau\cdot\sqrt{\alpha}\zeta_{6}\sqrt{2{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\alpha^{2}C_{3}\left(C_{9}\frac{\alpha\tau}{\sqrt{{\rm NewA}}}+C_{10}\alpha^{2}+6A_{1}\alpha\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Delta_{k}]\right)

(
\sqrt{.}
is concave,
\zeta_{6}>0
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8C_{1}^{2}C_{3}^{2}u_{cm}^{2}}{\zeta_{6}^{2}}\alpha^{5}\tau^{2}+\alpha\zeta_{6}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})]+C_{3}C_{9}\frac{\alpha^{3}\tau}{\sqrt{{\rm NewA}}}+C_{3}C_{10}\alpha^{4}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+6A_{1}C_{3}\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{5}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha\Delta_{k}^{2}\right]

(Young’s inequality)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\frac{8C_{1}^{2}C_{3}^{2}u_{cm}^{2}}{\zeta_{6}^{2}}}_{C_{11}}\alpha^{5}\tau^{2}+\alpha\zeta_{6}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})]+C_{3}C_{9}\frac{\alpha^{3}\tau}{\sqrt{{\rm NewA}}}+\alpha^{4}[C_{3}C_{10}+3A_{1}C_{3}\alpha(\tau+1)]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+3\alpha A_{1}C_{3}\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{k}]

(By (
72
))

\displaystyle\leq C_{11}\alpha^{5}\tau^{2}+\alpha\zeta_{6}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})]+\frac{1}{2}C_{3}^{2}C_{9}^{2}\alpha^{4}\tau+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\alpha^{2}\tau}{{\rm NewA}}+\alpha^{4}[C_{3}C_{10}+3A_{1}C_{3}\alpha(\tau+1)]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+3\alpha A_{1}C_{3}\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{k}].

(50)

Furthermore, using Lemma
B.7
, (
72
) and Proposition
B.1
, the term in (
30
) can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle C_{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 16\tau^{2}\alpha^{2}C_{1}^{2}C_{4}u_{cm}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]+8\alpha^{2}A_{1}^{2}C_{4}\tau\sum_{k=0}^{\tau}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[\Omega_{t-k}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{8C_{4}u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}}_{C_{12}}\frac{\alpha^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}\tau^{2}+\underbrace{\frac{8C_{4}u_{cD}^{2}Bm_{4}}{l_{cD}^{2}(1-\rho)}}_{C_{13}}\alpha^{4}\tau.

(51)

Inserting the upper bounds in (
50
) and (
51
) in the upper bound in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1-\alpha\left[2{\varphi}_{2}-\zeta_{1}^{2}-\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{2LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}-\zeta_{3}^{2}\frac{L(A_{1}+1)}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}-\zeta_{4}^{2}-\zeta_{6}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\alpha^{2}\frac{6Lu_{cm}^{2}}{l_{cs}^{2}\psi}\left[m_{1}+\frac{(A_{2}+1)^{2}}{2}\right]+\alpha^{2}16\tau^{2}C_{1}^{2}C_{4}u_{cm}^{2}\bigg){\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha C_{5}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{t}]+\alpha C_{6}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{t-\tau}]

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left(C_{7}+C_{11}\alpha\tau^{2}+0.5C_{3}^{2}C_{9}^{2}\tau+C_{3}C_{10}+3A_{1}C_{3}\alpha(\tau+1)+C_{13}\tau+C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}Bm_{4}\alpha^{2}\right)}_{C_{14}^{\prime}(\tau)}\alpha^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}+\frac{\tau}{2}+C_{12}\tau^{2}\right)\frac{\alpha^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}+\left(3\alpha A_{1}C_{3}+8\alpha^{2}A_{1}^{2}C_{4}\tau\right)\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{k}].

(52)

We define
C_{14}^{\prime}(\tau)\leq C_{14}(\tau)\triangleq\left(C_{7}+C_{11}+0.5C_{3}^{2}C_{9}^{2}+C_{3}C_{10}+3A_{1}C_{3}+C_{13}+C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}Bm_{4}\alpha^{2}\right)\tau^{2}
. Also, we choose
\zeta_{1}=\zeta_{4}=\zeta_{6}=\sqrt{{\varphi}_{2}/10}
,
\zeta_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{{\varphi}_{2}}{10}\cdot\frac{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}{2LA_{2}}}
,
\zeta_{3}=\sqrt{\frac{{\varphi}_{2}}{10}\cdot\frac{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}{L(A_{1}+1)}}
, and denote

\displaystyle C_{15}(\tau)=\left(\frac{6m_{1}Lu_{cm}^{2}}{l_{cs}^{2}\psi}+\frac{6L(A_{2}+1)^{2}u_{cm}^{2}}{2\psi l_{cs}^{2}}+144(A_{2}^{2}+1)C_{4}u_{cm}^{2}\right)\tau^{2}.

(53)

This yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right]\leq\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha{\varphi}_{2}+\alpha^{2}C_{15}(\tau)\right){\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]+\left(C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}+\frac{\tau}{2}+C_{12}\tau^{2}\right)\frac{\alpha^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{4}+(3\alpha A_{1}C_{3}+8\alpha^{2}A_{1}^{2}C_{4}\tau+\alpha C_{5}+\alpha C_{6})\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha{\varphi}_{2}+\alpha^{2}C_{15}(\tau)\right){\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}\left[M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]+C_{14}(\tau)\alpha^{4}+C_{16}(\tau)\frac{\alpha^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}+\alpha C_{17}\sum_{k=t-\tau}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-2\tau}[\Omega_{k}],

(54)

where
C_{16}(\tau)=\left(C_{8}\frac{u_{cD}^{2}B^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}+C_{12}\right)\tau^{2}
and
C_{17}=(3A_{1}C_{3}+8A_{1}^{2}C_{4}+C_{5}+C_{6})
. Due to
\alpha\leq\frac{{\varphi}_{2}}{2C_{15}(\tau)}
, we have
1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha{\varphi}_{2}+\alpha^{2}C_{15}(\tau)\leq 1-\alpha{\varphi}_{2}
. This completes the proof.

B.5
Proof of Proposition
B.4

First, we state the following lemma, which characterizes a bound on the expectation of the synchronization error
{\Omega_{t}}
.

Lemma B.8
.

Suppose Assumptions
3.1
,
3.3
holds and the step size
\alpha
satisfies
\alpha\leq\sqrt{\frac{\ln(5/4)}{2(1+\tilde{A}_{1})(K-1)^{2}}}
.
Then, for
sK\leq t\leq(s+1)K-1
, where
s=\lfloor t/K\rfloor
, the network consensus error
{\Omega_{t}}\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{i}\|_{c}^{2}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}{\Omega_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 5\alpha^{2}(t-sK)\tilde{B}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}+\frac{4m_{3}(t-sK-1)}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)+5\alpha^{2}(t-sK)\tilde{A}_{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=sK}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t^{\prime}}\right\|_{c}^{2},

(55)

where
\tilde{A}_{1}=\frac{2A_{1}^{2}u_{c2}^{2}}{l_{c2}^{2}},\tilde{A}_{2}=\frac{2A_{2}^{2}u_{c2}^{2}}{l_{c2}^{2}},\tilde{B}=\frac{u_{c2}}{l_{c2}}B
.
Here,
A_{1},A_{2},B
are the constants defined in Assumption
3.3
, and

l_{c2},u_{c2}
are constants involved in the equivalence of the norms:
l_{c2}\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\cdot\right\|_{c}\leq u_{c2}\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}
.

Due to the assumption on step size, the bound in Lemma
B.8
holds.
Substituting the bound on
\Omega_{\ell}
from Lemma
B.8
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\left[\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Omega_{\ell}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\bigg[5\alpha^{2}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\tilde{B}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}+\frac{4m_{3}(l-1-sK)}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+5\alpha^{2}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\tilde{A}_{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}\mathbb{E}\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t^{\prime}}\right\|_{c}^{2}\bigg].

(56)

where
s_{\ell}=\lfloor\ell/K\rfloor
.
Hence,
s_{\ell}K
denotes the last time instant before
\ell
when synchronization occurred. The first term in (
56
) can be upper bounded as follows.

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}5\alpha^{2}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\tilde{B}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}+\frac{4m_{3}(\ell-1-s_{\ell}K)}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}\right)\frac{10C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\left(\frac{4m_{3}}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)\frac{10C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)(\ell-1-s_{\ell}K)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}\right)\frac{10C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(K-1)

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\left(\frac{m_{3}}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)\frac{40C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(K-1)^{2}

(since
\ell-s_{\ell}K\leq K-1
)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}\right)\frac{10C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left[\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\right](\tau+1)(K-1)

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\left(\frac{m_{3}}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)\frac{40C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left[\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\right](\tau+1)(K-1)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}+\frac{4m_{3}(K-1)}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)\left[\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\right]\frac{10C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}(\tau+1)(K-1).

(57)

Next, we compute the second term in (
56
).

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}5\alpha^{2}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\tilde{A}_{2}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\right\|_{c}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})

(Proposition
B.1
)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\left[\underbrace{\sum_{t=2\tau}^{K}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{t=K+1}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\right],

(58)

where if
K<2\tau
,
\mathcal{I}_{1}=0
.
Next, we bound
\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{2}
separately.

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=2\tau}^{K}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)\sum_{t=2\tau}^{K}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})

(for
\ell<K
,
s_{\ell}=0
; for
\ell=K
,
\ell-s_{\ell}K=0
)

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)(\tau+1)\sum_{t=2\tau}^{K}w_{t}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{t-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)(K-2\tau+1)(\tau+1)w_{K}\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}),

(59)

where, (
59
) follows since
w_{t-1}\leq w_{t},\forall\ t
.
Next, to bound
\mathcal{I}_{2}
in (
58
), we again split it into two terms.

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}=\underbrace{\sum_{t=K+1}^{K+\tau}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})}_{\mathcal{I}_{3}}+\underbrace{\sum_{t=K+\tau+1}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})}_{\mathcal{I}_{4}}.

First, we bound
\mathcal{I}_{3}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}\leq\sum_{t=K+1}^{K+\tau}w_{t}\left[\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{K}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+\sum_{\ell=K+1}^{t}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=K+1}^{K+\tau}w_{t}\left[\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{K}\ell\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+\sum_{\ell=K+1}^{t}(\ell-K)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})\right]

(
s_{\ell}=0
for
\ell<K
,
s_{\ell}\geq 1
for
K\leq\ell\leq K+\tau
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=K+1}^{K+\tau}w_{t}\left[K(K+1-t+\tau)\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+(t-K)^{2}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=K}^{t-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq w_{K+\tau}K\tau^{2}\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})+\tau^{3}w_{K+\tau}\sum_{t=K}^{K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}).

(60)

Next, we bound
\mathcal{I}_{4}
, assuming
t_{0}K+\tau\leq T<(t_{0}+1)K+\tau
, where
t_{0}
is a non-negative integer.

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{4}=\sum_{t=K+\tau+1}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\underbrace{(\ell-s_{\ell}K)}_{\leq K-1}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)\sum_{t=K+\tau+1}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=(K-1)\left[w_{K+\tau+1}\sum_{\ell=K+1}^{K+\tau+1}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+\cdots+w_{K+\tau+K-1}\sum_{\ell=2K-1}^{2K+\tau-1}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(K-1)\left[w_{2K+\tau}\sum_{\ell=2K}^{2K+\tau}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+\cdots+w_{3K+\tau-1}\sum_{\ell=3K-1}^{3K+\tau-1}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\cdots+(K-1)\left[w_{t_{0}K+\tau}\sum_{\ell=t_{0}K}^{t_{0}K+\tau}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+\cdots+w_{T}\sum_{\ell=T-\tau}^{T}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)(\tau+1)\left[w_{K+\tau+1}\sum_{\ell=K}^{K+\tau}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+\cdots+w_{2K+\tau-1}\sum_{\ell=K}^{2K+\tau-2}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(K-1)(\tau+1)\left[w_{2K+\tau}\sum_{\ell=2K}^{2K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+\cdots+w_{3K+\tau-1}\sum_{\ell=2K}^{3K+\tau-2}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\cdots+(K-1)(\tau+1)\left[w_{t_{0}K+\tau}\sum_{\ell=t_{0}K}^{t_{0}K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+\cdots+w_{T}\sum_{\ell=t_{0}K}^{T-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\right]

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)K(\tau+1)\left[w_{2K+\tau-1}\sum_{\ell=K}^{2K+\tau-2}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+w_{3K+\tau-1}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=2K}^{3K+\tau-2}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+\cdots+w_{T}\sum_{\ell=t_{0}K}^{T-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\right]

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)K(\tau+1)\Bigg[w_{2K+\tau-1}\left(\sum_{\ell=K}^{2K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+\sum_{\ell=2K}^{2K+\tau-2}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+w_{3K+\tau-1}\left(\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=2K}^{3K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=3K}^{3K+\tau-2}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\cdots+w_{t_{0}K+\tau-1}\left(\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=(t_{0}-1)K}^{t_{0}K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=t_{0}K}^{t_{0}K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}})\right)+w_{T}\sum_{\ell=t_{0}K}^{T-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)K(\tau+1)\left\lceil\frac{(\tau-1)}{K}\right\rceil\Bigg[\sum_{\ell=K}^{2K-1}w_{\ell+K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+\sum_{\ell=2K}^{3K-1}w_{\ell+K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+\cdots+w_{T}\sum_{\ell=t_{0}K}^{T-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)\tau(\tau+1)\Bigg[\sum_{\ell=K}^{T-K-\tau}w_{\ell+K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+w_{T}\sum_{\ell=T-K-\tau+1}^{T-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\Bigg]

\displaystyle=(K-1)\tau(\tau+1)\Bigg[\sum_{\ell=K}^{T-K-\tau}\frac{w_{\ell}}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K+\tau-1}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})+\sum_{\ell=T-K-\tau+1}^{T-1}\frac{w_{\ell}}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{T-\ell}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{\ell})\Bigg]

(
w_{t}=w_{t+1}\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(K-1)\tau(\tau+1)}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K+\tau-1}}\sum_{t=K}^{T-1}w_{t}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}).

(61)

Using the bounds on
\mathcal{I}_{3},\mathcal{I}_{4}
from (
60
) and (
61
) respectively, we can bound
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq w_{K+\tau}K\tau^{2}\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})+\tau^{3}w_{K+\tau}\sum_{t=K}^{K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})+\frac{(K-1)\tau(\tau+1)}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K+\tau-1}}\sum_{t=K}^{T-1}w_{t}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}).

(62)

Substituting the bounds on
\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{2}
from (
59
), (
62
) respectively, into (
58
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}5\alpha^{2}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\tilde{A}_{2}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\right\|_{c}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\left[(K-1)(K-2\tau+1)(\tau+1)w_{K}\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})+K\tau^{2}w_{K+\tau}\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\Bigg[\tau^{3}w_{K+\tau}\sum_{t=K}^{K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})+\frac{(K-1)\tau(\tau+1)}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K+\tau-1}}\sum_{t=K}^{T-1}w_{t}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\Bigg]

(63)

We analyze the terms in (
63
) separately. First, for the terms with
\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{W_{T}}\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}}K(\tau+1)\left[(K-2\tau+1)w_{K}+\tau w_{K+\tau}\right]\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{W_{T}}\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}}K(\tau+1)\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}\left[(K-2\tau+1)\frac{w_{t}}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K-t}}+\tau\frac{w_{t}}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K+\tau-t}}\right]M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{W_{T}}\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}}K(\tau+1)\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}\left[(K-2\tau+1)+\frac{\tau}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{\tau}}\right]\frac{w_{t}}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K-t}}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}}K(\tau+1)\left[(K-2\tau+1)+\frac{\tau}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{\tau}}\right]\frac{1}{W_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}w_{t}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2W_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}w_{t}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}),\qquad\qquad\qquad

(64)

where (
64
) holds since we choose
\alpha
small enough such that

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\left[(K-2\tau+1)+\frac{\tau}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{\tau}}\right]K(\tau+1)\leq\frac{1}{2}.

To get this, we use the inequality
1-x\geq\exp{\left(-\frac{x}{1-x}\right)}
for
x<1
,
\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\tau<2\log_{\rho}\alpha+1
, we get
\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{\tau}}\leq\exp\left(\alpha{\varphi}_{2}\left(2\log_{\rho}\alpha+1\right)\right)
. For (
64
) to hold, it is sufficient that

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\leq\frac{{\varphi}_{2}}{80C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}K2(\log_{\rho}(\alpha)+1)}\min\left\{\frac{1}{K},\frac{1}{2(\log_{\rho}(\alpha)+1)\exp\left(\alpha{\varphi}_{2}\left(2\log_{\rho}\alpha+1\right)\right)}\right\}

(65)

Next, for the remaining terms in the third line of (
63
), by the assumption on the step size, we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\frac{\tau^{3}}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{\tau}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4},

(66)

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\frac{(\tau+1)\tau(K-1)}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K+\tau-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4},

and hence we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{W_{T}}\alpha^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}\frac{20C_{17}u_{cm}^{2}}{{\varphi}_{2}}\Bigg[\frac{\tau^{3}w_{K}}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{\tau}}\sum_{t=K}^{K+\tau-1}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})+\frac{(\tau+1)\tau(K-1)}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha{\varphi}_{2}}{2}\right)^{K+\tau-1}}\sum_{t=K}^{T}w_{t}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2W_{T}}\sum_{t=K}^{T}w_{t}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}).

(67)

Substituting (
64
), (
67
) in (
63
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}5\alpha^{2}(\ell-s_{\ell}K)\tilde{A}_{2}\sum_{t=s_{\ell}K}^{\ell-1}\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\|_{c}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2W_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}w_{t}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}).

(68)

Finally, substituting the bounds in (
57
), (
68
) into (
56
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}W_{T}}\sum_{t=2\tau}^{T}w_{t}\left[\sum_{\ell=t-\tau}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Omega_{\ell}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\left(1+\frac{4(m_{2}+m_{4})\rho}{B(1-\rho)}+\frac{4m_{3}(K-1)}{BN}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{i}\right)\frac{10C_{17}}{{\varphi}_{2}}(\tau+1)(K-1)+\frac{1}{2W_{T}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}w_{t}\mathbb{E}M({\bm{\theta}}_{t}).

(69)

B.6
Auxiliary Lemmas

The following lemma is of central importance in proving the linear speedup of FeGSAM.

Lemma B.9
.

Let
l_{cD}
and
u_{cD}
be constants that satisfy
l_{cD}\|\cdot\|_{D}\leq\|\cdot\|_{c}\leq u_{cD}\|\cdot\|_{D}
, where
\|\cdot\|_{D}=\sqrt{x^{\top}Dx}
for some positive definite matrix
D\succ 0
. Note that for any
D\succ 0
, these constants always exist due to norm equivalence. Furthermore, in case the norm
\|x\|_{c}
is defined in the form
\sqrt{x^{\top}Dx}
for some
D\succ 0
, we take
l_{cD}=u_{cD}=1
. We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-r}[\|\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{u_{cD}}{l_{cD}}\left[\frac{B}{\sqrt{N}}+\sqrt{Bm_{4}}\rho^{r/2}\right]

(70)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-r}[\|\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{u_{cD}^{2}}{l_{cD}^{2}}\left[\frac{B^{2}}{{\rm NewA}}+Bm_{4}\rho^{r}\right].

(71)

Lemma
B.9
is essential in characterizing the linear speedup in Theorem
B.1
. This lemma characterizes the bound on the conditional expectation of
\|\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}
and
\|\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}
, conditioned on the
r
time steps before. In order to understand this lemma, consider the bound in (
71
). For the sake of understanding, suppose that the noise
{\mathbf{Y}_{t}}
is i.i.d. In this case, we will end up with the first term which is proportional to
1/{\rm NewA}
. This is precisely the linear reduction of the variance of sum of
{\rm NewA}
i.i.d. random variables. Furthermore, in order to extend the i.i.d. noise setting to the more general Markovian noise, we need to pay an extra price by adding the exponentially decreasing term to the first variance term.

Lemma B.10
.

The following hold

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-\mathbf{G}(\bm{{\bm{\theta}}}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|^{2}_{c}\leq A_{1}^{2}\Delta_{t}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle A_{1}^{2}\Omega_{t}

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}^{2}\leq\Omega_{t}.

(72)

Lemma B.11
.

For the generalized Moreau Envelope defined in (
26
), it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla{M}_{f}^{\psi,g}(x)\right\|_{m}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\left\|x\right\|_{m},

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla{M}_{f}^{\psi,g}(x),x\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq 2{M}_{f}^{\psi,g}(x).

B.7
Proof of Lemmas

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}),{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}})-{\bm{\theta}}\right\rangle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}})\right\|_{m}^{\star}\cdot\left\|{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}})\right\|_{m}}_{T_{11}}-\underbrace{\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}),{\bm{\theta}}\right\rangle}_{T_{12}},

(73)

where
\|\cdot\|_{m}^{\star}
denotes the dual of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{m}
. Furthermore, by Proposition
B.1
we have

\displaystyle T_{11}\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\theta}}\|_{m}.\|{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}})\|_{m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\theta}}\|_{m}.l_{cm}^{-1}\left\|\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}})\right\|_{c}

(By Proposition (
B.1
))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\theta}}\|_{m}.l_{cm}^{-1}\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\left\|{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}})\right\|_{c}

(triangle inequality)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\theta}}\|_{m}.\frac{u_{cm}\gamma_{c}}{l_{cm}}\|{\bm{\theta}}\|_{m}

(Assumption
3.2
, Proposition
B.1
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2u_{cm}\gamma_{c}}{l_{cm}}M({\bm{\theta}}).

(74)

Furthermore, by the convexity of the
\|\cdot\|_{m}
norm (Lemma
B.11
), we have

\displaystyle T_{12}\geq\|{\bm{\theta}}\|^{2}_{m}=2{M}({\bm{\theta}}).

(75)

Hence, using (
74
), (
75
) in (
73
) we get

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}),{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}({\bm{\theta}})-{\bm{\theta}}\right\rangle\leq-2\left(1-\frac{u_{cm}\gamma_{c}}{l_{cm}}\right)M({\bm{\theta}})=-2{\varphi}_{2}M({\bm{\theta}}),

(76)

where
{\varphi}_{2}
is defined in (
28
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
B.2
.

Given some
\tau<t
,
T_{2}=\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\rangle
can be written as follows:

\displaystyle T_{2}=

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})-\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})-\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})\|_{s}^{\star}.\|b({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{s}+\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L}{\psi l_{cs}}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}.\frac{1}{l_{cs}}\|b({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}+\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\alpha}\left(\frac{L}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\right)^{2}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|b({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}+\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right\rangle.

(77)

Taking expectation on both sides, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[T_{2}]=

\displaystyle\frac{L^{2}}{2\alpha\psi^{2}l_{cs}^{4}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|b({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}+\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\left[\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L^{2}}{2\alpha\psi^{2}l_{cs}^{4}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|b({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}+\underbrace{\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})\|_{s}^{\star}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\left[\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right]\|_{s}}_{T_{21}}.

(78)

For
T_{21}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{21}\leq

\displaystyle l_{cs}^{-1}\left[\left\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})\right\|_{s}^{\star}\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\left[\mathbf{b}({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\right]\right\|_{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle l_{cs}^{-1}[\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})\|_{s}^{\star}m_{4}\rho^{\tau}]

(Assumption
3.1
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}\alpha^{2}}{l_{cs}}\left[\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})-\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\|_{s}^{\star}+\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t})\|_{s}^{\star}\right]

(assumption on
\tau
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}\alpha^{2}}{l_{cs}}\left[\frac{L}{\psi}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{s}+\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t})-\nabla{M}(\mathbf{0})\|_{s}^{\star}\right]

(Proposition
B.1
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}\alpha^{2}}{l_{cs}^{2}}\frac{L}{\psi}[\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{c}+\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{c}]

(By (
27
))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}\alpha}{l_{cs}^{2}}\frac{L}{\psi}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{c}+\frac{m_{4}\alpha^{2}u_{cm}}{\zeta_{1}l_{cs}^{2}}\frac{L}{\psi}\cdot\zeta_{1}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{m}

(Proposition
B.1
;
\zeta_{1}>0
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}\alpha}{l_{cs}^{2}}\frac{L}{\psi}\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\|_{c}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{m_{4}\alpha^{2}u_{cm}}{\zeta_{1}l_{cs}^{2}}\frac{L}{\psi}\right)^{2}+\zeta_{1}^{2}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}}).

(79)

Substituting (
79
) in (
78
), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[T_{2}]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L^{2}}{2\alpha\psi^{2}l_{cs}^{4}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|b({\mathbf{Y}_{t}})\|_{c}^{2}+\frac{\alpha m_{4}L}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|_{c}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{m_{4}\alpha^{2}u_{cm}}{\zeta_{1}l_{cs}^{2}}\frac{L}{\psi}\right)^{2}+\zeta_{1}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{t-\tau}[M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})].

∎

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.

\displaystyle T_{3}=

\displaystyle\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}}),\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t})\rangle

(80)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})-\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t})\rangle}_{T_{31}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t-\tau},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})+{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t-\tau})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t})\rangle}_{T_{32}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),\mathbf{G}(\bm{\Theta}_{t-\tau},{\mathbf{Y}_{t}})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bm{\Theta}_{t-\tau})\rangle}_{T_{33}}.

Next, we bound all three terms individually.

I. Bound on
T_{31}
:

\displaystyle T_{31}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})-\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})-\bar{\mathbf{G}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\underbrace{\left\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})-\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})\right\|_{s}^{\star}}_{T_{311}}\cdot\underbrace{\left\|{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})-\bar{\mathbf{G}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}})\right\|_{s}}_{T_{312}}.

(81)

For
T_{311}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{311}=

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}_{t}})-\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})\right\|_{s}^{\star}\leq\frac{L}{\psi l_{cs}}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c},

(82)

where the inequality follows from Proposition
B.1
and (
27
). For
T_{312}
, we have,

\displaystyle T_{312}=

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}\sim\mu^{i}}\mathbf{G}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}})\|_{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle l_{cs}^{-1}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})\right\|_{c}+{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}\sim\mu^{i}}\left\|\mathbf{G}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}})\right\|_{c}\right]

(Triangle and Jensen’s inequality)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle l_{cs}^{-1}[A_{2}\|{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}}\|_{c}+A_{2}\|{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}}\|_{c}]

(Assumption
3.3
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2A_{2}}{l_{cs}}\left[\|{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\|_{c}+\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\|_{c}\right]

(triangle inequality)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2A_{2}}{l_{cs}}\left[\Delta^{i}_{t}+\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\|_{c}\right].

(83)

Substituting (
82
) and (
83
) in (
81
), we get

\displaystyle T_{31}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}\cdot\left[\Delta^{i}_{t}+\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\|_{c}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\left[\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}\cdot\Delta^{i}_{t}\right]+\frac{u_{cm}}{\zeta_{2}}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}\cdot\zeta_{2}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\|_{m}\right]

(Proposition
B.1
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\left[\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\Delta^{i}_{t})^{2}\right]+\frac{u_{cm}^{2}}{2\zeta_{2}^{2}}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}+\frac{\zeta_{2}^{2}}{2}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\|_{m}^{2}\right]

(Young’s inequality)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2LA_{2}}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{u_{cm}^{2}}{2\zeta_{2}^{2}}\right)\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}^{2}+\zeta_{2}^{2}M({\bm{\theta}_{t}})+\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{t}\right].

(84)

II. Bound on
T_{32}
:

\displaystyle T_{32}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\left\langle\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}),{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})-{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})+{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\underbrace{\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})\|_{s}^{\star}}_{T_{321}}\cdot\underbrace{\|{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})-{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})+{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}})\|_{s}}_{T_{322}},

(85)

where the last inequality is by Hölder’s inequlaity. For
T_{321}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{321}=

\displaystyle\|\nabla{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau})-\nabla{M}(\mathbf{0})\|_{s}^{\star}\leq\frac{L}{\psi}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}-\mathbf{0}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L}{\psi l_{cs}}\left[\left\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\right\|_{c}+\left\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\right\|_{c}\right].

(86)

For
T_{322}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{322}=

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})-{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})+{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}})\|_{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{cs}}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})-{\mathbf{G}^{i}}({\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau},{\mathbf{y}^{i}_{t}})\right\|_{c}+\left\|{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau})-{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}^{i}({\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}})\right\|_{c}\right]

(triangle inequality)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{cs}}\left[{A_{1}}\|{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}+\gamma_{c}\|{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{A_{1}+1}{l_{cs}}\left[\left\|{\bm{\theta}^{i}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\right\|_{c}+\left\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\right\|_{c}+\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}-{\bm{\theta}}^{i}_{t-\tau}\right\|_{c}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{A_{1}+1}{l_{cs}}\left[\Delta_{t}^{i}+\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}+\Delta_{t-\tau}^{i}\right].

(87)

Substituting (
86
) and (
87
) in (
85
), we get

\displaystyle T_{32}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L(A_{1}+1)}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\Bigg[\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\zeta_{3}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\|_{c}\cdot\frac{1}{\zeta_{3}}\left[\Delta_{t}^{i}+\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}+\Delta_{t-\tau}^{i}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}\cdot\left[\Delta_{t}^{i}+\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}+\Delta_{t-\tau}^{i}\right]\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L(A_{1}+1)}{\psi l_{cs}^{2}}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{3}^{2}\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}\|_{m}^{2}+\frac{1}{2{\rm NewA}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rm NewA}\frac{u_{cm}^{2}}{\zeta_{3}^{2}}\left[\Delta_{t}^{i}+\|{\bm{\theta}_{t}}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-\tau}\|_{c}+\Delta_{t-\tau}^{i}\right]^{2}
